---
title: "kubernetes_core_pod"
description: "`kubernetes_core_pod` is the smallest real, deployable unit in Kubernetes -- one or more containers that share the same network namespace and storage..."
---

`kubernetes_core_pod` is the smallest real, deployable unit in Kubernetes -- one or more containers that share the same network namespace and storage, always scheduled, started, and stopped together as a single real thing.

## Example

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Go">
    ```go
    package main

    import (
    	ubx "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-go/runtime"
    	core "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-kubernetes/sdk/go/kubernetes/core"
    )

    func main() {
    	ubx.Main(ubx.Stack("example", func() {
    		ubx.Intent(ubx.IntentInfo{Summary: "example own pod"})

    		ubx.Resource(core.Pod, "example", core.PodConfig{
    			Name:      "example",
    			Namespace: "example",
    		})
    	}))
    }
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="TypeScript">
    ```typescript
    import { intent, resource, stack } from "@ubx/sdk";
    import { Pod } from "@ubx/sdk-kubernetes/kubernetes/core/pod";

    export default stack("example", () => {
      intent({ summary: "example own pod" });

      resource(Pod, "example", {
        name: "example",
        namespace: "example",
      });
    });
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Python">
    ```python
    import ubx_sdk as ubx
    from ubx.kubernetes.core.pod import Pod, PodConfig

    def describe():
        ubx.intent("example own pod")

        ubx.resource(Pod, "example", PodConfig(
            name="example",
            namespace="example",
        ))

    if __name__ == "__main__":
        ubx.run("example", describe)
    ```
  </Tab>
  <Tab title="Markdown">
    ```
    Create kubernetes_core_pod called "example", with namespace
    example.
    ```
  </Tab>
</Tabs>

      Must be empty before the object is deleted from the registry. Each entry is an identifier for the responsible component that will remove the entry from the list. If the deletionTimestamp of the object is non-nil, entries in this list can only be removed. Finalizers may be processed and removed in any order. Order is NOT enforced because it introduces significant risk of stuck finalizers. finalizers is a shared field, any actor with permission can reorder it. If the finalizer list is processed in order, then this can lead to a situation in which the component responsible for the first finalizer in the list is waiting for a signal (field value, external system, or other) produced by a component responsible for a finalizer later in the list, resulting in a deadlock. Without enforced ordering finalizers are free to order amongst themselves and are not vulnerable to ordering changes in the list. Optional.

<ResponseField name="spec" type="object">
  PodSpec is a description of a pod. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="active_deadline_seconds" type="number">
      Optional duration in seconds the pod may be active on the node relative to StartTime before the system will actively try to mark it failed and kill associated containers. Value must be a positive integer. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="affinity" type="object">
      Affinity is a group of affinity scheduling rules. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="node_affinity" type="object">
          Node affinity is a group of node affinity scheduling rules. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="preferred_during_scheduling_ignored_during_execution" type="list(object)">
              The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and adding "weight" to the sum if the node matches the corresponding matchExpressions; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="preference" type="object">
                  The node selector term that defines the node selection criteria for this preferred scheduling preference. It contains matchExpressions and matchFields, which are used to match nodes based on labels and fields. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="match_expressions" type="list(object)">
                      Specifies a list of node selector requirements that constrain eligible nodes by labels. Each requirement defines a key, an operator (e.g., In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist, Gt, Lt), and optionally value(s) that must be matched. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
                          The Kubernetes label key that the match expression applies to. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                          The operator to apply when matching the key against the listed values. Valid operators are In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist, Gt, and Lt. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">
                          A list of values for the node selector expression. Required when the operator is In or NotIn; must be empty for Exists or DoesNotExist. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="match_fields" type="list(object)">
                      A list of node selector requirements that match against node fields (e.g., metadata.name, spec.unschedulable) rather than node labels. Each requirement contains a key, operator, and optional values. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="key" type="string">

                        <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">

            <ResponseField name="required_during_scheduling_ignored_during_execution" type="list(object)">
              If the affinity requirements specified by this field are not met at scheduling time, the pod will not be scheduled onto the node. If the affinity requirements specified by this field cease to be met at some point during pod execution (e.g. due to a pod label update), the system may or may not try to eventually evict the pod from its node. When there are multiple elements, the lists of nodes corresponding to each podAffinityTerm are intersected, i.e. all terms must be satisfied. Optional.

The selector matches pods whose labels satisfy all requirements. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**

                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                          The operator represents the relationship between the label key and the set of values in a label selector requirement. Valid values are In, NotIn, Exists, and DoesNotExist. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">
                          The list of values that the label selector key must match, depending on the operator. For operators In and NotIn, this list must be non-empty; for Exists and DoesNotExist, it must be empty. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="match_labels" type="map(string)">
                      A map of label keys and values used to select pods that match all specified labels. This selector is used by pod affinity to identify the set of pods that the affinity rule applies to. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**

      evictionResponders reference responders that react to Evictions based on EvictionRequests. Responders should observe and communicate through the Eviction Resource API to help with the graceful termination of a pod. The responders are selected sequentially, according to their specified priority. Responders should periodically report on an eviction progress by updating the .status.responders[].heartbeatTime field of the Eviction object. If this field is not updated within the heartbeat deadline defined by the Eviction API (currently 20 minutes), the eviction is passed over to the next responder with a lower priority. If there is no other responder, the last default imperative-eviction.k8s.io/evictor responder with a priority of 100 will evict the pod using the imperative Eviction API (pods/&lt;name&gt;/eviction subresource). The maximum length of the responders list is 10. Responders are not supported when the pod is part of a PodGroup (.spec.schedulingGroup is set). This field can only be set on creation and is immutable afterwards. Optional.

        <ResponseField name="priority" type="number">

        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>

When `hostNetwork` is true, specified `hostPort` fields in port definitions must match `containerPort`, and unspecified `hostPort` fields in port definitions are defaulted to match `containerPort`. Default to false. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="host_pid" type="bool">
      Use the host's pid namespace. Optional: Default to false. Optional.
    </ResponseField>

If set to true or not present, the pod will be run in the host user namespace, useful for when the pod needs a feature only available to the host user namespace, such as loading a kernel module with CAP_SYS_MODULE. When set to false, a new userns is created for the pod. Setting false is useful for mitigating container breakout vulnerabilities even allowing users to run their containers as root without actually having root privileges on the host. Optional.

When this field is set to a non-empty string: - It takes precedence over the values set in `hostname` and `subdomain`. - The Pod's hostname will be set to this value. - `setHostnameAsFQDN` must be nil or set to false. - `hostNetwork` must be set to false. This field must be a valid DNS subdomain as defined in RFC 1123 and contain at most 64 characters. Optional.

The resourceRequirements of an init container are taken into account during scheduling by finding the highest request/limit for each resource type, and then using the max of that value or the sum of the normal containers. Optional.

        <ResponseField name="se_linux_change_policy" type="string">
          seLinuxChangePolicy defines how the container's SELinux label is applied to all volumes used by the Pod. It has no effect on nodes that do not support SELinux or to volumes does not support SELinux. Valid values are "MountOption" and "Recursive". "Recursive" means relabeling of all files on all Pod volumes by the container runtime. This may be slow for large volumes, but allows mixing privileged and unprivileged Pods sharing the same volume on the same node. "MountOption" mounts all eligible Pod volumes with `-o context` mount option. This requires all Pods that share the same volume to use the same SELinux label. It is not possible to share the same volume among privileged and unprivileged Pods. Eligible volumes are in-tree FibreChannel and iSCSI volumes, and all CSI volumes whose CSI driver announces SELinux support by setting spec.seLinuxMount: true in their CSIDriver instance. Other volumes are always re-labelled recursively. If not specified, "MountOption" is used. This field affects only Pods that have SELinux label set, either in PodSecurityContext or in SecurityContext of all containers. All Pods that use the same volume should use the same seLinuxChangePolicy, otherwise some pods can get stuck in ContainerCreating state. Note that this field cannot be set when spec.os.name is windows. Optional.

            <ResponseField name="role" type="string">

            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="type" type="string">
              Type is a SELinux type label that applies to the container. Optional.
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="user" type="string">

            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="type" type="string" required>

If the SupplementalGroupsPolicy feature is enabled, the supplementalGroupsPolicy field determines whether these are in addition to or instead of any group memberships defined in the container image. If unspecified, no additional groups are added, though group memberships defined in the container image may still be used, depending on the supplementalGroupsPolicy field. Note that this field cannot be set when spec.os.name is windows. Optional.

In Linux containers, this means setting the FQDN in the hostname field of the kernel (the nodename field of struct utsname). In Windows containers, this means setting the registry value of hostname for the registry key HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\\SYSTEM\\CurrentControlSet\\Services\\Tcpip\\Parameters to FQDN. If a pod does not have FQDN, this has no effect. Default to false. Optional.

When this is set containers will be able to view and signal processes from other containers in the same pod, and the first process in each container will not be assigned PID 1. HostPID and ShareProcessNamespace cannot both be set. Optional: Default to false. Optional.

The grace period is the duration in seconds after the processes running in the pod are sent a termination signal and the time when the processes are forcibly halted with a kill signal.             </ResponseField>

              <Expandable title="properties">

</ResponseField>

<ResponseField name="status" type="object">
  PodStatus represents information about the status of a pod. Status may trail the actual state of a system, especially if the node that hosts the pod cannot contact the control plane. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
  <Expandable title="properties">
    <ResponseField name="allocated_resources" type="map(string)">
      AllocatedResources is the total requests allocated for this pod by the node. If pod-level requests are not set, this will be the total requests aggregated across containers in the pod. Optional.
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="conditions" type="list(object)">
      Current service state of pod. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle#pod-conditions Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="last_probe_time" type="string">
          The timestamp (RFC3339) of the last time this condition was probed. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="last_transition_time" type="string">
          Last time the condition transitioned from one status to another. This is typically represented as an RFC3339 timestamp string. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="message" type="string">
          Human-readable message indicating details about the condition's last transition. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="observed_generation" type="number">
          Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="reason" type="string">
          A machine-readable, CamelCase reason for the condition's last transition. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="status" type="string">
          The status of the condition, indicating whether the condition is currently True, False, or Unknown. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="type" type="string">
          The type of pod condition, typically one of PodScheduled, Ready, Initialized, or ContainersReady. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
      </Expandable>
    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="container_statuses" type="list(object)">
      Statuses of containers in this pod. Each container in the pod should have at most one status in this list, and all statuses should be for containers in the pod. However this is not enforced. If a status for a non-existent container is present in the list, or the list has duplicate names, the behavior of various Kubernetes components is not defined and those statuses might be ignored. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle#pod-and-container-status Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="allocated_resources" type="map(string)">
          The resources allocated to the container, such as CPU and memory, expressed as a map of resource names to the allocated quantities (in string form). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="allocated_resources_status" type="list(object)">
          Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="name" type="string">
              The name of the resource to which this status applies, such as cpu, memory, or a named device. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
            </ResponseField>
            <ResponseField name="resources" type="list(object)">
              A list of health statuses for the resources allocated to the container, where each entry includes a resource ID and its health condition (e.g., Healthy, Unhealthy). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="health" type="string">
                  Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes.
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="message" type="string">
                  A human-readable message providing details about the status of the resource claim allocated to the container, such as an error or warning message. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="resource_id" type="string">

      nominatedNodeName is set only when this pod preempts other pods on the node, but it cannot be scheduled right away as preemption victims receive their graceful termination periods. This field does not guarantee that the pod will be scheduled on this node. Scheduler may decide to place the pod elsewhere if other nodes become available sooner. Scheduler may also decide to give the resources on this node to a higher priority pod that is created after preemption. As a result, this field may be different than PodSpec.nodeName when the pod is scheduled. Optional.

    </ResponseField>
    <ResponseField name="phase" type="string">
      The phase of a Pod is a simple, high-level summary of where the Pod is in its lifecycle. The conditions array, the reason and message fields, and the individual container status arrays contain more detail about the pod's status. There are five possible phase values: Pending: The pod has been accepted by the Kubernetes system, but one or more of the container images has not been created. This includes time before being scheduled as well as time spent downloading images over the network, which could take a while. Running: The pod has been bound to a node, and all of the containers have been created. At least one container is still running, or is in the process of starting or restarting. Succeeded: All containers in the pod have terminated in success, and will not be restarted. Failed: All containers in the pod have terminated, and at least one container has terminated in failure. The container either exited with non-zero status or was terminated by the system. Unknown: For some reason the state of the pod could not be obtained, typically due to an error in communicating with the host of the pod. More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle#pod-phase Optional.
    </ResponseField>

    </ResponseField>

PodResizePending will track states where the spec has been resized, but the Kubelet has not yet allocated the resources. PodResizeInProgress will track in-progress resizes, and should be present whenever allocated resources != acknowledged resources. Optional.

If Requests is omitted for a container, it defaults to Limits if that is explicitly specified, otherwise to an implementation-defined value. Requests cannot exceed Limits.
